  computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by

  output ports: result[64]

An unsigned design operand as wide as its port has no exact representation on this block.
  timing: the block is combinational; `result` is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied (no clock port).

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 under this run's VPR settings (regime 'koios_small': channel width 300, device koios_small, seed 1) has a critical path of 11.6069 ns and uses 224 DSP tiles of the device's 306, one block per tile. Its used logic-block area is 9.73358e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 1.12977e+09. Its slowest stage is in my_fir_f1: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then six adder levels with their carry chains, then one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop. The next slowest stage, in my_fir_f2 (endpoint group $dffe~642, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 11.293 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. These rules hold for any synchronous design on any FPGA. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone. A term of the original sum that goes on no block port (an addend beside the products) stays outside the block, added to its result. That holds when the module's instantiation ties the term to a constant: the module must compute what it computed for every value of its ports, and the flow flattens the design and removes an add of a constant, so the term puts nothing on the stage and rule 2 does not count it. Fold a term only when it is constant inside the module itself, and say so in the plan.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A block occupies one DSP tile in one mode. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Measured on this architecture

Observed across 12 timing reports through 2026-09-20; block, clock-to-Q and packing rows are from the architecture file and exact; wire and adder rows are sampled ranges with the median in parentheses.

Every DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; a block in either invented mode takes its tile alone.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

Read the source. Output EXACTLY this header (uppercase keys), then the plan:

PLAN: <how the module's live inputs/outputs and downstream consumers are preserved; any introduced
operations/datatypes (should be NONE for T1/T2); and the main risks>

Output rules: no preamble before the TIER line — read the source first, then write. You
are one step of an automated pipeline: a reviewer runs automatically on this plan, you
cannot invoke it, and no human reads your output, so do not end with a question or an
offer.

my_fir_h3 and my_fir_h4 (6, 3 blocks): (din,9 | n1,56), (n2,109 | n4,109), (n5,56 | n6,9)
PLAN:

What changes. In each of the seven my_fir_* modules the single dout <= (din*C0) + (n_delay_reg1*C1) + ... expression is re-expressed as comb_sop_2_18 instances plus a shallow soft adder tree, all inside the same if (new_data_rdy) branch of the same always @(posedge clk). Nothing else in the modules moves. Each module body is edited once; the 7 modules are instantiated once each inside h_fltr, and h_fltr is instantiated 6 times (lines 188-190, 195-197), so 22 block instances per h_fltr × 6 = 132 blocks.

Why these and not the others. The baseline's slowest stage (11.6069 ns) and its next-slowest (11.293 ns) are both in my_fir_*: hard multiply → six adder levels → LUT → enabled FF. Fixing only my_fir_f1 buys nothing past 11.293 (rule 1), so all seven my_fir_* modules get the same treatment; they are structurally identical and all sit at the same depth.

All 42 fltr_compute_* candidates (ids $44-$50, $59-$63, $72-$78, $86-$92, $101-$105, $114-$120, $128-$134) are left alone, one shared reason: their products are already registered into q1..q7 one stage before the sum, so the multiply stage and the sum stage are separate and neither is the critical stage (the sum stage carries six adder levels but no multiply and no block, ~4 ns below the my_fir stage). Fusing them would require pairing each multiply with its sum partner and re-registering partial sums (to keep the module's 3-cycle latency, rule 5), which converts 4-multiplies-per-tile 9x9 packing into 1-block-per-tile: 23 blocks per v_fltr × 6 = 138 tiles replacing ~57, i.e. roughly +81 tiles (~+2.1e7 area, +21%) for zero clock-period gain. That raises area-delay product, so rule 1's "paid for in tiles alone" rules it out.

Exactness. din and n_delay_reg* are wire/reg [15:0], unsigned, 16 bits — narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports, so zero-extension is exact. Every coefficient is a positive constant ≤ 255 (COEF*_b/_c/_d/_1/_2/_3/_4), far inside 19-bit signed range. The baseline does not truncate products before summing: the Verilog expression is evaluated at 32 bits and only the final sum is truncated to the 28-bit dout, so a fused sum is bit-identical. No wrap exists to reproduce: the largest coefficient sum is my_fir_f2/h2 at 673, and 65535×673 = 44,105,055 < 2²⁶ < 2²⁸. All terms are non-negative, so the block's signed 64-bit result equals the unsigned product-pair sum and result[27:0] is exact; the final tree is computed on 28-bit slices (keeps the soft carry chains at 28 bits, same width as today).

Preservation. dout is the only consumer of the sum (no internal sum node is named or read elsewhere), and it keeps its register, its new_data_rdy enable, and its latency of one cycle — the blocks are combinational and go in the existing combinational cone (rule 2: nothing but the reduction tree between block and register). output_data_ready and the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain are untouched; those registers are read by the chain itself, but as block operands they are exempt (the block takes a copy). Module ports, h_fltr, and steer_fltr are unchanged. Every input port of every block is driven; unused term ports are tied to 0.

Timing. Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — this mode has no addend/chain port, so blocks never feed blocks here. New stage, 4-block modules (f1): 0.06 clk-to-Q + 2.25 reg→block + 2.14 block + 1.48 block→soft + 2×1.08 adder levels = 8.09 ns. New stage, 3-block + 2-shift-term modules (f2, h2, five terms → 3 adder levels) and 3-block modules f3/h1/h3/h4 (3 terms → 2 levels, 7.01 ns): worst is 9.17 ns, which is the number reported above. Both are below the baseline's 11.6069 ns and below the 11.293 ns second path. What leaves the critical stage: four of the six soft adder levels (and three for f2/h2) plus the trailing LUT, replaced by the block's own adder, which is free inside the block (rule 4).

Area. Baseline packs these as two_mult_18x19, one or two to a tile. Per h_fltr: 41 multiplies; if packed per module that is 4+3+3+3+3+3+3 = 22 tiles, exactly the 22 blocks we instantiate (net 0, total 224). If the packer shares tiles across module boundaries it could be as few as ceil(41/2)=21 tiles, giving +1 per h_fltr, +6 overall. I report the pessimistic 230 of 306 tiles; 224 is the likely outcome. Flip-flop count is unchanged (no product registers exist in these modules and none are added, rule 5). Removed soft adder levels may free clusters, but per rule 4 no LUT saving is claimed.

ADP. At the pessimistic tile count, area ≤ 9.73358e7 + 6×253779 = 9.886e7. If the period lands at the 9.17 ns stage this plan leaves, ADP ≈ 9.1e8 vs the baseline 1.12977e9, ~20% better; even if some untouched stage limits at 11.0 ns, ADP ≈ 1.09e9, still below baseline.

Risks. (1) The period after the rewrite is set by whatever untouched stage is now slowest; the timing report only named the two my_fir paths, so the gain could be capped above 9.17 ns — but both named paths are fixed, so the period must fall below 11.293 ns. (2) If the packer had been sharing tiles across module boundaries, up to 6 tiles are added (+1.6% area); the delay gain dominates that. (3) Wire delays are sampled medians; the 8.09/9.17 figures carry roughly ±1 ns, still clear of 11.293. (4) Keeping the two *4 terms of f2/h2 outside the blocks costs one extra adder level there rather than 12 extra tiles — the deliberate choice, since those stages are not the limiter.

No new operations or datatypes are introduced; no constant-term folding is used.

The next slowest stage, in my_fir_f2 (endpoint group $dffe~642, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 11.293 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.
APPROVE the plan if it is correct: a legitimate adaptation does not need to be perfect, only sound.
Do NOT demand rewrites for style, minor wording, or hypothetical concerns.
REVISE ONLY for a CONCRETE, specific violation you can name: (a) it changes the computed function
when the tier forbids it; (b) it introduces an operation/datatype absent from the design and not
admitted by the tier; (c) it fabricates terms to match a mode's n_terms (products that did not
exist / do not share a reduction); (d) it wires the mode to constants or a dead sink (mode island).
If none of these apply, APPROVE.
Your FIRST line must be exactly 'APPROVE' or 'REVISE', nothing else. If REVISE, the next lines
must name the specific violation (a/b/c/d) and what to change.
Output rules: no preamble before the verdict line — verify first, then write. Your
verdict is applied automatically and no human reads it, so do not end with a question
or an offer.

SHARED_REDUCTION: the single dout sum of each my_fir_* module (one FIR accumulation per module; dout drives vidin_out_temp_* in h_fltr, lines 514-520)
BLOCKS: 132
TILES: 230
STAGE_NS: 9.17
MAPPING: per block, ax = {2'b00, <16-bit unsigned tap>} (zero-extend, 16 < 18), ay = 19'd<coef>, bx = {2'b00, <next tap>}, by = 19'd<next coef>; an odd leftover product takes bx=18'd0, by=19'd0; result[27:0] feeds the soft reduction. Pairings in source order:
my_fir_f1 (7 products, 4 blocks): (din,29 | n1,101), (n2,15 | n3,235), (n4,15 | n5,101), (n6,29 | 0,0)
my_fir_f2 and my_fir_h2 (5 products, 3 blocks): (n1,42 | n2,163), (n3,255 | n4,163), (n5,42 | 0,0); the two *4 terms (din, n6) are shifts, not multiplies, and stay outside as {din,2'b00} / {n6,2'b00}
my_fir_f3 (6, 3 blocks): (din,12 | n1,77), (n2,148 | n4,148), (n5,77 | n6,12)
my_fir_h1 (6, 3 blocks): (din,15 | n1,25), (n2,193 | n4,193), (n5,25 | n6,15)
my_fir_h3 and my_fir_h4 (6, 3 blocks): (din,9 | n1,56), (n2,109 | n4,109), (n5,56 | n6,9)
PLAN:

What changes. In each of the seven my_fir_* modules the single dout <= (din*C0) + (n_delay_reg1*C1) + ... expression is re-expressed as comb_sop_2_18 instances plus a shallow soft adder tree, all inside the same if (new_data_rdy) branch of the same always @(posedge clk). Nothing else in the modules moves. Each module body is edited once; the 7 modules are instantiated once each inside h_fltr, and h_fltr is instantiated 6 times (lines 188-190, 195-197), so 22 block instances per h_fltr × 6 = 132 blocks.

Why these and not the others. The baseline's slowest stage (11.6069 ns) and its next-slowest (11.293 ns) are both in my_fir_*: hard multiply → six adder levels → LUT → enabled FF. Fixing only my_fir_f1 buys nothing past 11.293 (rule 1), so all seven my_fir_* modules get the same treatment; they are structurally identical and all sit at the same depth.

All 42 fltr_compute_* candidates (ids $44-$50, $59-$63, $72-$78, $86-$92, $101-$105, $114-$120, $128-$134) are left alone, one shared reason: their products are already registered into q1..q7 one stage before the sum, so the multiply stage and the sum stage are separate and neither is the critical stage (the sum stage carries six adder levels but no multiply and no block, ~4 ns below the my_fir stage). Fusing them would require pairing each multiply with its sum partner and re-registering partial sums (to keep the module's 3-cycle latency, rule 5), which converts 4-multiplies-per-tile 9x9 packing into 1-block-per-tile: 23 blocks per v_fltr × 6 = 138 tiles replacing ~57, i.e. roughly +81 tiles (~+2.1e7 area, +21%) for zero clock-period gain. That raises area-delay product, so rule 1's "paid for in tiles alone" rules it out.

Exactness. din and n_delay_reg* are wire/reg [15:0], unsigned, 16 bits — narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports, so zero-extension is exact. Every coefficient is a positive constant ≤ 255 (COEF*_b/_c/_d/_1/_2/_3/_4), far inside 19-bit signed range. The baseline does not truncate products before summing: the Verilog expression is evaluated at 32 bits and only the final sum is truncated to the 28-bit dout, so a fused sum is bit-identical. No wrap exists to reproduce: the largest coefficient sum is my_fir_f2/h2 at 673, and 65535×673 = 44,105,055 < 2²⁶ < 2²⁸. All terms are non-negative, so the block's signed 64-bit result equals the unsigned product-pair sum and result[27:0] is exact; the final tree is computed on 28-bit slices (keeps the soft carry chains at 28 bits, same width as today).

Preservation. dout is the only consumer of the sum (no internal sum node is named or read elsewhere), and it keeps its register, its new_data_rdy enable, and its latency of one cycle — the blocks are combinational and go in the existing combinational cone (rule 2: nothing but the reduction tree between block and register). output_data_ready and the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain are untouched; those registers are read by the chain itself, but as block operands they are exempt (the block takes a copy). Module ports, h_fltr, and steer_fltr are unchanged. Every input port of every block is driven; unused term ports are tied to 0.

Timing. Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — this mode has no addend/chain port, so blocks never feed blocks here. New stage, 4-block modules (f1): 0.06 clk-to-Q + 2.25 reg→block + 2.14 block + 1.48 block→soft + 2×1.08 adder levels = 8.09 ns. New stage, 3-block + 2-shift-term modules (f2, h2, five terms → 3 adder levels) and 3-block modules f3/h1/h3/h4 (3 terms → 2 levels, 7.01 ns): worst is 9.17 ns, which is the number reported above. Both are below the baseline's 11.6069 ns and below the 11.293 ns second path. What leaves the critical stage: four of the six soft adder levels (and three for f2/h2) plus the trailing LUT, replaced by the block's own adder, which is free inside the block (rule 4).

Area. Baseline packs these as two_mult_18x19, one or two to a tile. Per h_fltr: 41 multiplies; if packed per module that is 4+3+3+3+3+3+3 = 22 tiles, exactly the 22 blocks we instantiate (net 0, total 224). If the packer shares tiles across module boundaries it could be as few as ceil(41/2)=21 tiles, giving +1 per h_fltr, +6 overall. I report the pessimistic 230 of 306 tiles; 224 is the likely outcome. Flip-flop count is unchanged (no product registers exist in these modules and none are added, rule 5). Removed soft adder levels may free clusters, but per rule 4 no LUT saving is claimed.

ADP. At the pessimistic tile count, area ≤ 9.73358e7 + 6×253779 = 9.886e7. If the period lands at the 9.17 ns stage this plan leaves, ADP ≈ 9.1e8 vs the baseline 1.12977e9, ~20% better; even if some untouched stage limits at 11.0 ns, ADP ≈ 1.09e9, still below baseline.

Risks. (1) The period after the rewrite is set by whatever untouched stage is now slowest; the timing report only named the two my_fir paths, so the gain could be capped above 9.17 ns — but both named paths are fixed, so the period must fall below 11.293 ns. (2) If the packer had been sharing tiles across module boundaries, up to 6 tiles are added (+1.6% area); the delay gain dominates that. (3) Wire delays are sampled medians; the 8.09/9.17 figures carry roughly ±1 ns, still clear of 11.293. (4) Keeping the two *4 terms of f2/h2 outside the blocks costs one extra adder level there rather than 12 extra tiles — the deliberate choice, since those stages are not the limiter.

No new operations or datatypes are introduced; no constant-term folding is used.

APPROVE

Verification against stereovision2.v:

  • Region census matches source. f1 has 7 $mul cells (lines 1486–1492); f2 and h2 have only 5 each because COEF0_c/COEF6_c and COEF0_2/COEF6_2 are 4 (power-of-two shifts, not $mul cells) — the plan identifies exactly this and keeps {din,2'b00} / {n6,2'b00} as soft addends; f3/h1/h3/h4 have 6 each (COEF3_* is 0 and absent from the source expression). Total 7+5+6+6+5+6+6 = 41, equal to the 41 selected regions.
  • Coefficient pairings are faithful. f1 (29,101|15,235|15,101|29,—), f2/h2 (42,163|255,163|42,—), f3 (12,77|148,148|77,12), h1 (15,25|193,193|25,15), h3/h4 (9,56|109,109|56,9) all match stereovision2.v:1440-1449, 1500-1506, 1561-1567, 1616-1625, 1675-1681, 1734-1740, 1792-1798.
  • (a) Function preserved. Operands are unsigned [15:0] (WIDTH_5B = 16), zero-extension into 18-bit ax/bx is exact; coefficients are small positive constants in 19-bit range. Max accumulated value is f2/h2's 65535×673 = 44,105,055 < 2²⁸, so the baseline 28-bit dout never wraps and regrouping the all-non-negative sum is bit-identical. The dout register, its new_data_rdy enable, the delay chain, and module ports are untouched.
  • (b) No new op/datatype. Products-summed-into-dout on int16 operands is the existing family; comb_sop_2_18 is the target mode.
  • (c) No fabricated terms. Every mapped product exists in the source and all terms in a module share the single dout accumulation.
  • (d) No mode island. Each block output feeds the live soft tree into dout, which drives vidin_out_temp_* and the registered vidin_out_reg_* into steer_fltr (stereovision2.v:514-521). The three half-used blocks (odd leftover in f1, f2, h2) still carry a real product on the other term pair; zero-padding an unused operand pair is not a constant-driven block.
  • Scale and budget are consistent: 22 blocks per h_fltr × 6 instances (lines 188–190, 195–197) = 132 blocks; 230 of 306 tiles under the pessimistic packing assumption.

Timing/area numbers are estimates and the plan states its uncertainty, which is not a reviewable violation.

my_fir_h3 and my_fir_h4 (6, 3 blocks): (din,9 | n1,56), (n2,109 | n4,109), (n5,56 | n6,9)
PLAN:

What changes. In each of the seven my_fir_* modules the single dout <= (din*C0) + (n_delay_reg1*C1) + ... expression is re-expressed as comb_sop_2_18 instances plus a shallow soft adder tree, all inside the same if (new_data_rdy) branch of the same always @(posedge clk). Nothing else in the modules moves. Each module body is edited once; the 7 modules are instantiated once each inside h_fltr, and h_fltr is instantiated 6 times (lines 188-190, 195-197), so 22 block instances per h_fltr × 6 = 132 blocks.

Why these and not the others. The baseline's slowest stage (11.6069 ns) and its next-slowest (11.293 ns) are both in my_fir_*: hard multiply → six adder levels → LUT → enabled FF. Fixing only my_fir_f1 buys nothing past 11.293 (rule 1), so all seven my_fir_* modules get the same treatment; they are structurally identical and all sit at the same depth.

All 42 fltr_compute_* candidates (ids $44-$50, $59-$63, $72-$78, $86-$92, $101-$105, $114-$120, $128-$134) are left alone, one shared reason: their products are already registered into q1..q7 one stage before the sum, so the multiply stage and the sum stage are separate and neither is the critical stage (the sum stage carries six adder levels but no multiply and no block, ~4 ns below the my_fir stage). Fusing them would require pairing each multiply with its sum partner and re-registering partial sums (to keep the module's 3-cycle latency, rule 5), which converts 4-multiplies-per-tile 9x9 packing into 1-block-per-tile: 23 blocks per v_fltr × 6 = 138 tiles replacing ~57, i.e. roughly +81 tiles (~+2.1e7 area, +21%) for zero clock-period gain. That raises area-delay product, so rule 1's "paid for in tiles alone" rules it out.

Exactness. din and n_delay_reg* are wire/reg [15:0], unsigned, 16 bits — narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports, so zero-extension is exact. Every coefficient is a positive constant ≤ 255 (COEF*_b/_c/_d/_1/_2/_3/_4), far inside 19-bit signed range. The baseline does not truncate products before summing: the Verilog expression is evaluated at 32 bits and only the final sum is truncated to the 28-bit dout, so a fused sum is bit-identical. No wrap exists to reproduce: the largest coefficient sum is my_fir_f2/h2 at 673, and 65535×673 = 44,105,055 < 2²⁶ < 2²⁸. All terms are non-negative, so the block's signed 64-bit result equals the unsigned product-pair sum and result[27:0] is exact; the final tree is computed on 28-bit slices (keeps the soft carry chains at 28 bits, same width as today).

Preservation. dout is the only consumer of the sum (no internal sum node is named or read elsewhere), and it keeps its register, its new_data_rdy enable, and its latency of one cycle — the blocks are combinational and go in the existing combinational cone (rule 2: nothing but the reduction tree between block and register). output_data_ready and the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain are untouched; those registers are read by the chain itself, but as block operands they are exempt (the block takes a copy). Module ports, h_fltr, and steer_fltr are unchanged. Every input port of every block is driven; unused term ports are tied to 0.

Timing. Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — this mode has no addend/chain port, so blocks never feed blocks here. New stage, 4-block modules (f1): 0.06 clk-to-Q + 2.25 reg→block + 2.14 block + 1.48 block→soft + 2×1.08 adder levels = 8.09 ns. New stage, 3-block + 2-shift-term modules (f2, h2, five terms → 3 adder levels) and 3-block modules f3/h1/h3/h4 (3 terms → 2 levels, 7.01 ns): worst is 9.17 ns, which is the number reported above. Both are below the baseline's 11.6069 ns and below the 11.293 ns second path. What leaves the critical stage: four of the six soft adder levels (and three for f2/h2) plus the trailing LUT, replaced by the block's own adder, which is free inside the block (rule 4).

Area. Baseline packs these as two_mult_18x19, one or two to a tile. Per h_fltr: 41 multiplies; if packed per module that is 4+3+3+3+3+3+3 = 22 tiles, exactly the 22 blocks we instantiate (net 0, total 224). If the packer shares tiles across module boundaries it could be as few as ceil(41/2)=21 tiles, giving +1 per h_fltr, +6 overall. I report the pessimistic 230 of 306 tiles; 224 is the likely outcome. Flip-flop count is unchanged (no product registers exist in these modules and none are added, rule 5). Removed soft adder levels may free clusters, but per rule 4 no LUT saving is claimed.

ADP. At the pessimistic tile count, area ≤ 9.73358e7 + 6×253779 = 9.886e7. If the period lands at the 9.17 ns stage this plan leaves, ADP ≈ 9.1e8 vs the baseline 1.12977e9, ~20% better; even if some untouched stage limits at 11.0 ns, ADP ≈ 1.09e9, still below baseline.

Risks. (1) The period after the rewrite is set by whatever untouched stage is now slowest; the timing report only named the two my_fir paths, so the gain could be capped above 9.17 ns — but both named paths are fixed, so the period must fall below 11.293 ns. (2) If the packer had been sharing tiles across module boundaries, up to 6 tiles are added (+1.6% area); the delay gain dominates that. (3) Wire delays are sampled medians; the 8.09/9.17 figures carry roughly ±1 ns, still clear of 11.293. (4) Keeping the two *4 terms of f2/h2 outside the blocks costs one extra adder level there rather than 12 extra tiles — the deliberate choice, since those stages are not the limiter.

No new operations or datatypes are introduced; no constant-term folding is used.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
====
`define COEF0_b  29
	`define COEF1_b  101
//	`define COEF2_b  -15
//	`define COEF3_b  -235
//	`define COEF4_b  -15
	`define COEF2_b  15
	`define COEF3_b  235
	`define COEF4_b  15
	`define COEF5_b  101
	`define COEF6_b  29

	`define WIDTH_5B 5'b10000
	
module my_fir_f1 (clk, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready, din, dout);

//coefdata=29,101,-15,-235,-15,101,29;

	//parameter `WIDTH = 5'b10000;

    input clk; 
    input[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0] din; 
	input new_data_rdy;
	output output_data_ready;
	reg output_data_ready;

    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg1;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg2;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg3;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg4;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg5;
    reg[`WIDTH_5B - 1:0]n_delay_reg6;

    always @(posedge clk)
    begin
		if (new_data_rdy == 1'b1)
		begin
			n_delay_reg1 <= din;
			n_delay_reg2 <= n_delay_reg1;
			n_delay_reg3 <= n_delay_reg2;
			n_delay_reg4 <= n_delay_reg3;
			n_delay_reg5 <= n_delay_reg4;
			n_delay_reg6 <= n_delay_reg5;
		
			output_data_ready <= 1'b1;
			dout <= (din * `COEF0_d) + 
				(n_delay_reg1 * `COEF1_d) + 
				(n_delay_reg2 * `COEF2_d) + 
				(n_delay_reg4 * `COEF4_d) + 
				(n_delay_reg5 * `COEF5_d) + 
				(n_delay_reg6 * `COEF6_d);
		end
		else
		begin
			output_data_ready <= 1'b0;
		end
	end
endmodule

	`define COEF0_1  15
//`define COEF0_1  -15
	`define COEF1_1  25
	`define COEF2_1  193
	`define COEF3_1  0

You are an INDEPENDENT ACCEPTANCE REVIEWER — the gate of record for whether an adapted design is
a LEGITIMATE member of its benchmark suite. You did NOT plan or write this edit. Judge only what
is in front of you. Your verdict is final and fully automated; there is no human backstop, so err
toward rejecting anything you cannot positively ground.

FRAMING — refute by default. Your job is to find why this adaptation is NOT a legitimate suite
member. Assume it is contrived until the evidence shows otherwise. A rewrite that merely "makes
the mode appear in the netlist" is NOT sufficient — the mode must do real work the original
workload actually implied.

This case has already passed the deterministic structural, provenance, and (for T3) evidence
gates — do not re-litigate those mechanically. Your judgement is the legitimacy call they
cannot make: would a real designer of THIS workload ship this, or is it a mode-island / fabricated
dot-product / gratuitous variant dressed up to pass?

REJECT if any of these hold:

  • (a) FABRICATED TERMS — a fused N-term product/sum aggregates operands that did not genuinely
    share a reduction in the original; the "dot product" is manufactured to fill the mode's arity.
  • (b) MODE ISLAND / DEAD WORK — the mode's result does not flow to the design's real outputs, or
    is wired to constants / a sink that the rest of the design ignores.
  • (c) ROLE BREAK — the adapted region no longer plays the workload role the witness recorded
    (e.g. a control-path multiply repurposed as a fake datapath MAC).
  • (d) UNGROUNDED VARIANT (T3) — the precision/algorithm variant is not the one the cited sibling
    design evidences; the adaptation drifts to a workload character the suite does not contain.

ACCEPT if the adaptation is sound: the mapped arithmetic genuinely existed, the fused terms really
shared a reduction, the mode's output drives the original consumers, and the workload role is
preserved. Legitimate adaptation need not be optimal — only real.

You MUST ground your verdict: cite the specific preserved role (from the witness), the real
region(s)/reduction the mode maps onto, and — for T3 — the sibling evidence axis. A verdict you
cannot ground is a REJECT.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision2.v

response

I'll examine the actual file before ruling.

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness → file). The witness records role my_fir_f1…h4, operation sop, int16, with the shared reduction being "the single dout sum of each my_fir_* module". In the edited file each of the seven my_fir_* modules is still a 7-tap transversal FIR: the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain is untouched (stereovision2.v:1507-1512, and the same block in each sibling), dout is still the single 28-bit accumulation registered under new_data_rdy, and output_data_ready still gates it. The datapath role — tap × coefficient products reduced into one FIR accumulator — is intact; nothing was repurposed from or into control logic (refutes (c)).

Real region mapped, no fabricated terms (refutes (a)). Every fused pair is two taps of the same dout sum, never operands from different modules or different reductions. Per-module the arithmetic is exactly the original:

  • my_fir_f1 (coefs 29,101,15,235,15,101,29 — none a power of two, so 7 $mul regions in the witness): 4 blocks cover taps 0–6, stereovision2.v:1486-1501.
  • my_fir_f3/h1/h3/h4 (COEF3 = 0, and the original source line for n_delay_reg3 is absent): 3 blocks cover exactly the 6 surviving taps, e.g. stereovision2.v:1955-1966 — the witness likewise lists 6 $mul regions for each.
  • my_fir_f2/h2 (COEF0 = COEF6 = 4): the ×4 taps stay as {din,2'b00} / {n_delay_reg6,2'b00} shifts and only the 5 genuine multiplies go into SOP blocks — matching the witness, which lists exactly 5 $mul regions for each of these two modules. 7+5+6+6+5+6+6 = 41 regions, the full witness set, with no residual soft * left anywhere in the FIR modules.

The three half-idle ports (sop_f1_i3, sop_f2_i2, sop_h2_i2 with bx/by = 0) are the ordinary consequence of mapping an odd tap count onto 2-term blocks, not padding to fake arity — each still carries a live ax*ay term into dout.

Output flow (refutes (b)). dout → vidin_out_temp_* at stereovision2.v:514-520 → vidin_out_reg_* at :527-552 → steer_fltr at :521, which produces real_z/imag_z/real_p/imag_p/real_n/imag_n. The mode results reach the design's real outputs through the original consumers listed in the witness. Width is lossless: 16-bit zero-extended taps × ≤8-bit positive coefficients give ≤25-bit block results, so the [27:0] slices into the 28-bit dout discard nothing.

(d) not applicable — T2, no precision or algorithm variant introduced (manifest: empty introduced operations/datatypes), so no sibling-evidence axis is required.

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 under this run's VPR settings (regime 'koios_small': channel width 300, device koios_small, seed 1) has a critical path of 11.6069 ns and uses 224 DSP tiles of the device's 306, one block per tile. Its used logic-block area is 9.73358e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 1.12977e+09. Its slowest stage is in my_fir_f1: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then six adder levels with their carry chains, then one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop. The next slowest stage, in my_fir_f2 (endpoint group $dffe~642, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 11.293 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. These rules hold for any synchronous design on any FPGA. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone. A term of the original sum that goes on no block port (an addend beside the products) stays outside the block, added to its result. That holds when the module's instantiation ties the term to a constant: the module must compute what it computed for every value of its ports, and the flow flattens the design and removes an add of a constant, so the term puts nothing on the stage and rule 2 does not count it. Fold a term only when it is constant inside the module itself, and say so in the plan.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A block occupies one DSP tile in one mode. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Every DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; a block in either invented mode takes its tile alone.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

Read the source. Output EXACTLY this header (uppercase keys), then the plan:

operations/datatypes (should be NONE for T1/T2); and the main risks>

Output rules: no preamble before the TIER line — read the source first, then write. You
are one step of an automated pipeline: a reviewer runs automatically on this plan, you
cannot invoke it, and no human reads your output, so do not end with a question or an

  • my_fir_f1 (coefs 29,101,15,235,15,101,29 — 7 taps, 4 blocks): B1 ax=din,ay=29,bx=n1,by=101; B2 ax=n2,ay=15,bx=n3,by=235; B3 ax=n4,ay=15,bx=n5,by=101; B4 ax=n6,ay=29,bx=0,by=0 (unused term ports tied to 0).

The *0 tap contributes nothing and is dropped as the baseline already drops it.
  • Reduction after the blocks, written as an explicit balanced tree (this is the change from the kept version): 4 summands → s1=B1+B2; s2=B3+B4_or_leftover; dout<=(s1+s2)[27:0]; 3 summands → s1=B1+B2; dout<=(s1+B3)[27:0]. Two soft adder levels on the block-to-register path instead of the four the left-associated source chain produced.

PLAN:

What changes relative to the kept result. The kept rewrite already put all 41 my_fir multiplies on 132 blocks (22 per module set × 6 instances) and measured 189 tiles / 10.0724 ns. The measured critical path shows why it stalled: comb_sop_2_18~1823 → $add~652 → $add~653 → $add~654 → $add~655 → one LUT → $dffe~662, i.e. four soft adder levels in series after the block. That is the source's left-associated a+b+c+d+e chain surviving synthesis, plus, in f2/h2, the two *4 shift taps appearing as two extra chain links. This revision keeps the identical block count, identical block wiring per product, and identical tile count, and only reassociates the shared reduction into a balanced tree plus folds one *4 shift tap onto an otherwise-zero product port. Block path depth drops 4 adder levels → 2.

Block/tile accounting. 132 blocks, exactly as measured today, each taking its tile alone: 22 per module set (f1 4; f2 3; f3 3; h1 3; h2 3; h3 3; h4 3) × 6 instances. Plus the 57 tiles the untouched fltr_compute 9x9 multiplies occupy = 189 tiles, 35 below the baseline's 224 and well under the device's 306. Zero tiles on top of the kept result, so the whole delay gain is free in area; area should land at or a hair below the measured 8.15609e7 (f2/h2 each shed one soft adder, and f1's B4 is unchanged).

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block feeds another: comb_sop_2_18 has no addend/chain port, every operand is a register output, and partial sums are 28-bit values that cannot be re-entered through an 18-bit ax port, so a series arrangement is ruled out by the arithmetic rule, not chosen against. Stage budget from the measured path elements: clock-to-Q 0.060 + register→block wire 2.253 + block 2.140 + wire into adder 0.752 + carry 0.110 + wire into adder 1.000 + carry 0.162 + wire into the final LUT 1.599 + LUT 0.153 + D wire 0.019 ≈ 8.25 ns, against the baseline's 11.6069 ns slowest stage and the kept 10.0724 ns. The two adder levels removed are exactly the soft logic that leaves the stage, and this stage (my_fir_f1/f2, endpoint group $dffe~456/$dffe~662) is the design's slowest one in both the baseline and the kept result, so rule 1 is satisfied: the rewrite touches the slowest stage and shortens it. The baseline's second-slowest stage, my_fir_f2 at 11.293 ns, is the same shape and is shortened by the same transformation applied uniformly to all seven my_fir modules, so there is no second-path floor at 11.29.

Insurance against the next stage becoming the limit. With my_fir at ~8.25 ns the next candidate is the fltr_compute_* d_out_tmp stage: seven registered 17-bit products sign-extended to 20 bits and summed by a six-deep left-associated chain, roughly 0.06 + ~1.0 wire + 6 × ~1.1 ≈ 7.7 ns. That is close enough to 8.25 to eat the gain. I therefore also rewrite that one expression in all seven fltr_compute modules as a balanced tree, ((e1+e2)+(e3+e4)) + ((e5+e6)+e7), depth 6 → 3, bringing the stage to roughly 4.5–5.0 ns. This instantiates no block and no tile: it is the same six 20-bit adders reassociated. Addition modulo 2^20 is associative, so the 20-bit register's wrap behaviour and every bit of d_out_tmp, hence dout <= d_out_tmp[18:3], are preserved exactly.

Exactness of the my_fir conversions. din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit unsigned, narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports → zero-extended, which is the allowed row of the operand table. Coefficients are positive constants (max 255) → zero-extended onto the 19-bit ay/by ports. No product is truncated before the sum in the baseline: the source multiplies and adds in the 28-bit context of dout, and the worst-case true sum is 65535 × 525 (f1's coefficient sum) ≈ 3.44e7 < 2^28, so nothing wraps and result[27:0] of the full-precision block sum equals the baseline bit for bit. The *0 tap in f3/h1/h3/h4 is a constant zero inside the module itself (the coefficient is a literal define, not a port), so dropping it is the fold the arithmetic rule permits; I state that explicitly. The *4 taps in f2/h2 are exact products of a constant and go on a product port with no loss; the one that does not fit a free port stays outside the blocks as a shift added to the tree, as the rule requires for a term that goes on no port.

Preserving inputs, outputs, consumers and registers. No register moves and no register is absorbed. The n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, its new_data_rdy enable, the output_data_ready register and the enabled 28-bit dout register are all untouched; the blocks sit purely combinationally between the existing delay registers and the existing dout flip-flop, so module latency, reset/enable behaviour and the dout/output_data_ready port interface are unchanged — the whole rewrite stays inside one stage. Observability: each n_delay_regK is also read by the next delay register, which is fine because operands are exempt (the block takes a copy); no value that any other expression, port or module reads is pulled inside a block, since the per-tap products din*COEFK have no reader other than this one sum. Both din fan-outs (into B1's ax and into n_delay_reg1) remain.

What I leave, and why. All 42 fltr_compute_* candidates (fltr_compute_f1 1486… sorry, ids 859$44, 860$45, 861$46, 862$47, 863$48, 864$49, 865$50; f2 890$59–894$63; f3 928$72, 929$73, 930$74, 932$76, 933$77, 934$78; h1 959$86, 960$87, 961$88, 963$90, 964$91, 965$92; h2 993$101–997$105; h3 1030$114, 1031$115, 1032$116, 1034$118, 1035$119, 1036$120; h4 1065$128–1071$134) stay on their existing 9x9 tiles, for two grouped reasons:

  1. Tile cost against a stage that is not and will not be the limit (all 42). These multiplies are packed four to a tile on 39 of 57 tiles. A fused block takes a tile alone, so converting them would need 22 blocks per module set × 6 instances = 132 additional tiles, taking the design to 321 tiles — past the device's 306 and far past any area the rewrite could repay. Their products are already registered one stage before the sum, so fusing them cannot shorten the sum stage at all; it would merely merge the product stage into the register, and with the sum stage rebalanced to ~5 ns there is nothing left to buy. Rule 1 (a rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone) and rule 6 rule them out.
  2. Inexact arithmetic, additionally, in fltr_compute_f1, f3 and h4. Each product is captured in a 17-bit q register and then read back as a signed 17-bit value via {q[16],q[16],q[16],q}. In f1 (coefficient 277 at tap 4), f3 (364) and h4 (324) the maximum product — 70,635 / 92,820 / 82,620 — exceeds 2^16, so q[16] is set and the design genuinely reinterprets that product as negative. A block's full-precision fused sum would produce the positive value instead. That is the "original truncates/reinterprets each product before the sum" row of the operand table: not representable, so these are excluded on correctness as well as on area. (In f2, h1, h2 and h3 the maximum products — 65,025 / 49,215 / 65,025 / 37,485 — stay below 2^16 and a fusion would be exact; they are still left, for reason 1 alone.)

Introduced operations or datatypes: NONE. No new operation family, no datatype change, no width change on any port or register; only reassociation of existing additions and the placement of existing products onto block ports.

Risks. (i) The estimate assumes the placer gives the rebalanced tree routing comparable to the measured path; the 1.599 ns wire into the final .names cell and the 2.253 ns register→block wire are routing-dominated and could shift, and seed noise on this design is ~5.9%, so a measured period around 8.3–9.0 ns rather than 8.25 is plausible. Even at 9.0 ns the product is ≈7.3e8, about 11% below the kept 8.215e8, so the change survives a full seed-noise swing. (ii) The floor is whatever third stage lies below the kept 10.07 ns; I cannot see it from here, and if it sits near 9 ns the gain is capped there rather than at 8.25 — the fltr_compute rebalance removes the most likely such stage at zero tile cost. (iii) The final .names cell between the last adder and $dffe (1.75 ns with its wire) is outside my control and is carried in the 8.25 ns figure.

I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1486$154, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1487$155, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1488$157, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1489$159, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1490$161, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1491$163, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1492$165, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1543$170, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1544$172, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1545$174, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1546$176, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1547$178, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1602$184, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1603$185, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1604$187, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1605$189, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1606$191, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1607$193, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1661$197, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1662$198, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1663$200, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1664$202, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1665$204, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1666$206, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1717$211, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1718$213, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1719$215, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1720$217, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1721$219, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1775$225, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1776$226, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1777$228, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1778$230, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1779$232, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1780$234, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1833$238, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1834$239, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1835$241, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1836$243, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1837$245, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1838$247
SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout <= (din*COEF0) + (n_delay_reg1*COEF1) + ... + (n_delay_reg6*COEF6) tap sum in each of my_fir_f1/f2/f3/h1/h2/h3/h4 (one shared 28-bit accumulator per module, feeding the enabled dout register)

TILES: 189
STAGE_NS: 8.25
MAPPING: per module instance, every tap n_delay_regK * COEFK becomes one product port pair; ax/bx take the 16-bit unsigned tap data zero-extended to 18 bits ({2'b0, din}, {2'b0, n_delay_regK}), ay/by take the positive constant coefficient zero-extended to 19 bits; result[27:0] is the summand entering the reduction tree.

  • my_fir_f1 (coefs 29,101,15,235,15,101,29 — 7 taps, 4 blocks): B1 ax=din,ay=29,bx=n1,by=101; B2 ax=n2,ay=15,bx=n3,by=235; B3 ax=n4,ay=15,bx=n5,by=101; B4 ax=n6,ay=29,bx=0,by=0 (unused term ports tied to 0).
  • my_fir_f2 (4,42,163,255,163,42,4 — 3 blocks): B1 ax=din,ay=4,bx=n1,by=42; B2 ax=n2,ay=163,bx=n3,by=255; B3 ax=n4,ay=163,bx=n5,by=42. The free din*4 tap is folded onto B1's ax port; the remaining n6*4 tap stays soft (a wire shift off a register).
  • my_fir_h2 (same coefs — 3 blocks): identical wiring to f2 with its own taps.
  • my_fir_f3 (12,77,148,0,148,77,12), my_fir_h1 (15,25,193,0,193,25,15), my_fir_h3 and my_fir_h4 (9,56,109,0,109,56,9) — 6 live taps each, 3 blocks each, no port tied off: B1 ax=din,ay=C0,bx=n1,by=C1; B2 ax=n2,ay=C2,bx=n4,by=C4; B3 ax=n5,ay=C5,bx=n6,by=C6. The *0 tap contributes nothing and is dropped as the baseline already drops it.
  • Reduction after the blocks, written as an explicit balanced tree (this is the change from the kept version): 4 summands → s1=B1+B2; s2=B3+B4_or_leftover; dout<=(s1+s2)[27:0]; 3 summands → s1=B1+B2; dout<=(s1+B3)[27:0]. Two soft adder levels on the block-to-register path instead of the four the left-associated source chain produced.

PLAN:

What changes relative to the kept result. The kept rewrite already put all 41 my_fir multiplies on 132 blocks (22 per module set × 6 instances) and measured 189 tiles / 10.0724 ns. The measured critical path shows why it stalled: comb_sop_2_18~1823 → $add~652 → $add~653 → $add~654 → $add~655 → one LUT → $dffe~662, i.e. four soft adder levels in series after the block. That is the source's left-associated a+b+c+d+e chain surviving synthesis, plus, in f2/h2, the two *4 shift taps appearing as two extra chain links. This revision keeps the identical block count, identical block wiring per product, and identical tile count, and only reassociates the shared reduction into a balanced tree plus folds one *4 shift tap onto an otherwise-zero product port. Block path depth drops 4 adder levels → 2.

Block/tile accounting. 132 blocks, exactly as measured today, each taking its tile alone: 22 per module set (f1 4; f2 3; f3 3; h1 3; h2 3; h3 3; h4 3) × 6 instances. Plus the 57 tiles the untouched fltr_compute 9x9 multiplies occupy = 189 tiles, 35 below the baseline's 224 and well under the device's 306. Zero tiles on top of the kept result, so the whole delay gain is free in area; area should land at or a hair below the measured 8.15609e7 (f2/h2 each shed one soft adder, and f1's B4 is unchanged).

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block feeds another: comb_sop_2_18 has no addend/chain port, every operand is a register output, and partial sums are 28-bit values that cannot be re-entered through an 18-bit ax port, so a series arrangement is ruled out by the arithmetic rule, not chosen against. Stage budget from the measured path elements: clock-to-Q 0.060 + register→block wire 2.253 + block 2.140 + wire into adder 0.752 + carry 0.110 + wire into adder 1.000 + carry 0.162 + wire into the final LUT 1.599 + LUT 0.153 + D wire 0.019 ≈ 8.25 ns, against the baseline's 11.6069 ns slowest stage and the kept 10.0724 ns. The two adder levels removed are exactly the soft logic that leaves the stage, and this stage (my_fir_f1/f2, endpoint group $dffe~456/$dffe~662) is the design's slowest one in both the baseline and the kept result, so rule 1 is satisfied: the rewrite touches the slowest stage and shortens it. The baseline's second-slowest stage, my_fir_f2 at 11.293 ns, is the same shape and is shortened by the same transformation applied uniformly to all seven my_fir modules, so there is no second-path floor at 11.29.

Insurance against the next stage becoming the limit. With my_fir at ~8.25 ns the next candidate is the fltr_compute_* d_out_tmp stage: seven registered 17-bit products sign-extended to 20 bits and summed by a six-deep left-associated chain, roughly 0.06 + ~1.0 wire + 6 × ~1.1 ≈ 7.7 ns. That is close enough to 8.25 to eat the gain. I therefore also rewrite that one expression in all seven fltr_compute modules as a balanced tree, ((e1+e2)+(e3+e4)) + ((e5+e6)+e7), depth 6 → 3, bringing the stage to roughly 4.5–5.0 ns. This instantiates no block and no tile: it is the same six 20-bit adders reassociated. Addition modulo 2^20 is associative, so the 20-bit register's wrap behaviour and every bit of d_out_tmp, hence dout <= d_out_tmp[18:3], are preserved exactly.

Exactness of the my_fir conversions. din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit unsigned, narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports → zero-extended, which is the allowed row of the operand table. Coefficients are positive constants (max 255) → zero-extended onto the 19-bit ay/by ports. No product is truncated before the sum in the baseline: the source multiplies and adds in the 28-bit context of dout, and the worst-case true sum is 65535 × 525 (f1's coefficient sum) ≈ 3.44e7 < 2^28, so nothing wraps and result[27:0] of the full-precision block sum equals the baseline bit for bit. The *0 tap in f3/h1/h3/h4 is a constant zero inside the module itself (the coefficient is a literal define, not a port), so dropping it is the fold the arithmetic rule permits; I state that explicitly. The *4 taps in f2/h2 are exact products of a constant and go on a product port with no loss; the one that does not fit a free port stays outside the blocks as a shift added to the tree, as the rule requires for a term that goes on no port.

Preserving inputs, outputs, consumers and registers. No register moves and no register is absorbed. The n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, its new_data_rdy enable, the output_data_ready register and the enabled 28-bit dout register are all untouched; the blocks sit purely combinationally between the existing delay registers and the existing dout flip-flop, so module latency, reset/enable behaviour and the dout/output_data_ready port interface are unchanged — the whole rewrite stays inside one stage. Observability: each n_delay_regK is also read by the next delay register, which is fine because operands are exempt (the block takes a copy); no value that any other expression, port or module reads is pulled inside a block, since the per-tap products din*COEFK have no reader other than this one sum. Both din fan-outs (into B1's ax and into n_delay_reg1) remain.

What I leave, and why. All 42 fltr_compute_* candidates (fltr_compute_f1 1486… sorry, ids 859$44, 860$45, 861$46, 862$47, 863$48, 864$49, 865$50; f2 890$59–894$63; f3 928$72, 929$73, 930$74, 932$76, 933$77, 934$78; h1 959$86, 960$87, 961$88, 963$90, 964$91, 965$92; h2 993$101–997$105; h3 1030$114, 1031$115, 1032$116, 1034$118, 1035$119, 1036$120; h4 1065$128–1071$134) stay on their existing 9x9 tiles, for two grouped reasons:

  1. Tile cost against a stage that is not and will not be the limit (all 42). These multiplies are packed four to a tile on 39 of 57 tiles. A fused block takes a tile alone, so converting them would need 22 blocks per module set × 6 instances = 132 additional tiles, taking the design to 321 tiles — past the device's 306 and far past any area the rewrite could repay. Their products are already registered one stage before the sum, so fusing them cannot shorten the sum stage at all; it would merely merge the product stage into the register, and with the sum stage rebalanced to ~5 ns there is nothing left to buy. Rule 1 (a rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone) and rule 6 rule them out.
  2. Inexact arithmetic, additionally, in fltr_compute_f1, f3 and h4. Each product is captured in a 17-bit q register and then read back as a signed 17-bit value via {q[16],q[16],q[16],q}. In f1 (coefficient 277 at tap 4), f3 (364) and h4 (324) the maximum product — 70,635 / 92,820 / 82,620 — exceeds 2^16, so q[16] is set and the design genuinely reinterprets that product as negative. A block's full-precision fused sum would produce the positive value instead. That is the "original truncates/reinterprets each product before the sum" row of the operand table: not representable, so these are excluded on correctness as well as on area. (In f2, h1, h2 and h3 the maximum products — 65,025 / 49,215 / 65,025 / 37,485 — stay below 2^16 and a fusion would be exact; they are still left, for reason 1 alone.)

Introduced operations or datatypes: NONE. No new operation family, no datatype change, no width change on any port or register; only reassociation of existing additions and the placement of existing products onto block ports.

Risks. (i) The estimate assumes the placer gives the rebalanced tree routing comparable to the measured path; the 1.599 ns wire into the final .names cell and the 2.253 ns register→block wire are routing-dominated and could shift, and seed noise on this design is ~5.9%, so a measured period around 8.3–9.0 ns rather than 8.25 is plausible. Even at 9.0 ns the product is ≈7.3e8, about 11% below the kept 8.215e8, so the change survives a full seed-noise swing. (ii) The floor is whatever third stage lies below the kept 10.07 ns; I cannot see it from here, and if it sits near 9 ns the gain is capped there rather than at 8.25 — the fltr_compute rebalance removes the most likely such stage at zero tile cost. (iii) The final .names cell between the last adder and $dffe (1.75 ns with its wire) is outside my control and is carried in the 8.25 ns figure.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision2.v

response

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verification against the source (/work/stereovision2.v):

  • Regions are real and complete. The 41 listed $mul ids line up with the tap multiplies at stereovision2.v:1486–1492 (f1, 7 taps), 1543–1547 (f2, 5 cells — the two *4 taps at 1542/1548 are shifts, as the plan says), 1602–1607 (f3), 1661–1666 (h1), 1717–1721 (h2), 1775–1780 (h3), 1833–1838 (h4). All feed the single 28-bit dout <= ... tap sum in their module — one genuine shared reduction per module, live consumer is the enabled dout register, which the plan leaves in place.
  • Coefficients match the COEF*_b/_c/_d/_1/_2/_3/_4 defines at lines 1440–1798 exactly (f1 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2/h2 4,42,163,255,163,42,4; f3 12,77,148,0,148,77,12; h1 15,25,193,0,193,25,15; h3/h4 9,56,109,0,109,56,9). The *0 taps are not merely dropped by the plan — the source itself never writes them (stereovision2.v:1602-1607, 1833-1838 skip n_delay_reg3), so nothing is lost.
  • (a) No function change. din/n_delay_regK are 16-bit unsigned (WIDTH_5B 5'b10000), coefficients are positive literals ≤255, and the sum is evaluated in 28-bit context. Worst case 65535×673 ≈ 4.4e7 < 2²⁸, so no wrap and the fused full-precision products equal the baseline bit for bit; reassociating the sum is exact. The extra fltr_compute change is also exact: all seven summands at e.g. stereovision2.v:867 are 20-bit sign-extended concats into a 20-bit d_out_tmp, and addition mod 2²⁰ is associative, so dout <= d_out_tmp[18:3] is preserved.
  • (b) No new op/datatype family. Only existing multiplies placed on the target comb_sop_2_18 ports plus reassociation of existing adds; no width or signedness change.
  • (c) No fabricated terms. Every product mapped to a port is an existing tap of the same reduction, including f2/h2's din*4 (a real term at line 1542 that synthesis turned into a shift). f1's B4 second slot is tied to 0 — an unused port, not an invented product; the remaining n6*4 stays soft and is explicitly kept in the tree.
  • (d) No mode island. Every block result enters the dout reduction; B4 carries a live n6*29 product.

The plan also addresses rule 1 (it shortens the actual slowest stage in f1 and the same-shaped f2 stage uniformly) and its tile/area estimates are self-consistent with 6 h_fltr instances (stereovision2.v:188-197). The fltr_compute tile/packing figures are an estimate rather than a verified count, and the extra fltr_compute rebalance is outside the selected regions, but neither is one of the named violations and both are function-preserving.

Two soft adder levels on the block-to-register path instead of the four the left-associated source chain produced.

PLAN:

What changes relative to the kept result. The kept rewrite already put all 41 my_fir multiplies on 132 blocks (22 per module set × 6 instances) and measured 189 tiles / 10.0724 ns. The measured critical path shows why it stalled: comb_sop_2_18~1823 → $add~652 → $add~653 → $add~654 → $add~655 → one LUT → $dffe~662, i.e. four soft adder levels in series after the block. That is the source's left-associated a+b+c+d+e chain surviving synthesis, plus, in f2/h2, the two *4 shift taps appearing as two extra chain links. This revision keeps the identical block count, identical block wiring per product, and identical tile count, and only reassociates the shared reduction into a balanced tree plus folds one *4 shift tap onto an otherwise-zero product port. Block path depth drops 4 adder levels → 2.

Block/tile accounting. 132 blocks, exactly as measured today, each taking its tile alone: 22 per module set (f1 4; f2 3; f3 3; h1 3; h2 3; h3 3; h4 3) × 6 instances. Plus the 57 tiles the untouched fltr_compute 9x9 multiplies occupy = 189 tiles, 35 below the baseline's 224 and well under the device's 306. Zero tiles on top of the kept result, so the whole delay gain is free in area; area should land at or a hair below the measured 8.15609e7 (f2/h2 each shed one soft adder, and f1's B4 is unchanged).

Longest series of blocks in any stage. One. No block feeds another: comb_sop_2_18 has no addend/chain port, every operand is a register output, and partial sums are 28-bit values that cannot be re-entered through an 18-bit ax port, so a series arrangement is ruled out by the arithmetic rule, not chosen against. Stage budget from the measured path elements: clock-to-Q 0.060 + register→block wire 2.253 + block 2.140 + wire into adder 0.752 + carry 0.110 + wire into adder 1.000 + carry 0.162 + wire into the final LUT 1.599 + LUT 0.153 + D wire 0.019 ≈ 8.25 ns, against the baseline's 11.6069 ns slowest stage and the kept 10.0724 ns. The two adder levels removed are exactly the soft logic that leaves the stage, and this stage (my_fir_f1/f2, endpoint group $dffe~456/$dffe~662) is the design's slowest one in both the baseline and the kept result, so rule 1 is satisfied: the rewrite touches the slowest stage and shortens it. The baseline's second-slowest stage, my_fir_f2 at 11.293 ns, is the same shape and is shortened by the same transformation applied uniformly to all seven my_fir modules, so there is no second-path floor at 11.29.

Insurance against the next stage becoming the limit. With my_fir at ~8.25 ns the next candidate is the fltr_compute_* d_out_tmp stage: seven registered 17-bit products sign-extended to 20 bits and summed by a six-deep left-associated chain, roughly 0.06 + ~1.0 wire + 6 × ~1.1 ≈ 7.7 ns. That is close enough to 8.25 to eat the gain. I therefore also rewrite that one expression in all seven fltr_compute modules as a balanced tree, ((e1+e2)+(e3+e4)) + ((e5+e6)+e7), depth 6 → 3, bringing the stage to roughly 4.5–5.0 ns. This instantiates no block and no tile: it is the same six 20-bit adders reassociated. Addition modulo 2^20 is associative, so the 20-bit register's wrap behaviour and every bit of d_out_tmp, hence dout <= d_out_tmp[18:3], are preserved exactly.

Exactness of the my_fir conversions. din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit unsigned, narrower than the 18-bit ax/bx ports → zero-extended, which is the allowed row of the operand table. Coefficients are positive constants (max 255) → zero-extended onto the 19-bit ay/by ports. No product is truncated before the sum in the baseline: the source multiplies and adds in the 28-bit context of dout, and the worst-case true sum is 65535 × 525 (f1's coefficient sum) ≈ 3.44e7 < 2^28, so nothing wraps and result[27:0] of the full-precision block sum equals the baseline bit for bit. The *0 tap in f3/h1/h3/h4 is a constant zero inside the module itself (the coefficient is a literal define, not a port), so dropping it is the fold the arithmetic rule permits; I state that explicitly. The *4 taps in f2/h2 are exact products of a constant and go on a product port with no loss; the one that does not fit a free port stays outside the blocks as a shift added to the tree, as the rule requires for a term that goes on no port.

Preserving inputs, outputs, consumers and registers. No register moves and no register is absorbed. The n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, its new_data_rdy enable, the output_data_ready register and the enabled 28-bit dout register are all untouched; the blocks sit purely combinationally between the existing delay registers and the existing dout flip-flop, so module latency, reset/enable behaviour and the dout/output_data_ready port interface are unchanged — the whole rewrite stays inside one stage. Observability: each n_delay_regK is also read by the next delay register, which is fine because operands are exempt (the block takes a copy); no value that any other expression, port or module reads is pulled inside a block, since the per-tap products din*COEFK have no reader other than this one sum. Both din fan-outs (into B1's ax and into n_delay_reg1) remain.

What I leave, and why. All 42 fltr_compute_* candidates (fltr_compute_f1 1486… sorry, ids 859$44, 860$45, 861$46, 862$47, 863$48, 864$49, 865$50; f2 890$59–894$63; f3 928$72, 929$73, 930$74, 932$76, 933$77, 934$78; h1 959$86, 960$87, 961$88, 963$90, 964$91, 965$92; h2 993$101–997$105; h3 1030$114, 1031$115, 1032$116, 1034$118, 1035$119, 1036$120; h4 1065$128–1071$134) stay on their existing 9x9 tiles, for two grouped reasons:

  1. Tile cost against a stage that is not and will not be the limit (all 42). These multiplies are packed four to a tile on 39 of 57 tiles. A fused block takes a tile alone, so converting them would need 22 blocks per module set × 6 instances = 132 additional tiles, taking the design to 321 tiles — past the device's 306 and far past any area the rewrite could repay. Their products are already registered one stage before the sum, so fusing them cannot shorten the sum stage at all; it would merely merge the product stage into the register, and with the sum stage rebalanced to ~5 ns there is nothing left to buy. Rule 1 (a rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone) and rule 6 rule them out.
  2. Inexact arithmetic, additionally, in fltr_compute_f1, f3 and h4. Each product is captured in a 17-bit q register and then read back as a signed 17-bit value via {q[16],q[16],q[16],q}. In f1 (coefficient 277 at tap 4), f3 (364) and h4 (324) the maximum product — 70,635 / 92,820 / 82,620 — exceeds 2^16, so q[16] is set and the design genuinely reinterprets that product as negative. A block's full-precision fused sum would produce the positive value instead. That is the "original truncates/reinterprets each product before the sum" row of the operand table: not representable, so these are excluded on correctness as well as on area. (In f2, h1, h2 and h3 the maximum products — 65,025 / 49,215 / 65,025 / 37,485 — stay below 2^16 and a fusion would be exact; they are still left, for reason 1 alone.)

Introduced operations or datatypes: NONE. No new operation family, no datatype change, no width change on any port or register; only reassociation of existing additions and the placement of existing products onto block ports.

Risks. (i) The estimate assumes the placer gives the rebalanced tree routing comparable to the measured path; the 1.599 ns wire into the final .names cell and the 2.253 ns register→block wire are routing-dominated and could shift, and seed noise on this design is ~5.9%, so a measured period around 8.3–9.0 ns rather than 8.25 is plausible. Even at 9.0 ns the product is ≈7.3e8, about 11% below the kept 8.215e8, so the change survives a full seed-noise swing. (ii) The floor is whatever third stage lies below the kept 10.07 ns; I cannot see it from here, and if it sits near 9 ns the gain is capped there rather than at 8.25 — the fltr_compute rebalance removes the most likely such stage at zero tile cost. (iii) The final .names cell between the last adder and $dffe (1.75 ns with its wire) is outside my control and is carried in the 8.25 ns figure.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

module my_fir_f1 (clk, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready, din, dout);

//coefdata=29,101,-15,-235,-15,101,29;
