You are an INDEPENDENT ACCEPTANCE REVIEWER — the gate of record for whether an adapted design is
a LEGITIMATE member of its benchmark suite. You did NOT plan or write this edit. Judge only what
is in front of you. Your verdict is final and fully automated; there is no human backstop, so err
toward rejecting anything you cannot positively ground.

FRAMING — refute by default. Your job is to find why this adaptation is NOT a legitimate suite
member. Assume it is contrived until the evidence shows otherwise. A rewrite that merely "makes
the mode appear in the netlist" is NOT sufficient — the mode must do real work the original
workload actually implied.

This case has already passed the deterministic structural, provenance, and (for T3) evidence
gates — do not re-litigate those mechanically. Your judgement is the legitimacy call they
cannot make: would a real designer of THIS workload ship this, or is it a mode-island / fabricated
dot-product / gratuitous variant dressed up to pass?

REJECT if any of these hold:

  • (a) FABRICATED TERMS — a fused N-term product/sum aggregates operands that did not genuinely
    share a reduction in the original; the "dot product" is manufactured to fill the mode's arity.
  • (b) MODE ISLAND / DEAD WORK — the mode's result does not flow to the design's real outputs, or
    is wired to constants / a sink that the rest of the design ignores.
  • (c) ROLE BREAK — the adapted region no longer plays the workload role the witness recorded
    (e.g. a control-path multiply repurposed as a fake datapath MAC).
  • (d) UNGROUNDED VARIANT (T3) — the precision/algorithm variant is not the one the cited sibling
    design evidences; the adaptation drifts to a workload character the suite does not contain.

ACCEPT if the adaptation is sound: the mapped arithmetic genuinely existed, the fused terms really
shared a reduction, the mode's output drives the original consumers, and the workload role is
preserved. Legitimate adaptation need not be optimal — only real.

You MUST ground your verdict: cite the specific preserved role (from the witness), the real
region(s)/reduction the mode maps onto, and — for T3 — the sibling evidence axis. A verdict you
cannot ground is a REJECT.

Your FIRST line must be exactly 'ACCEPT' or 'REJECT'. Then, on the following lines, state the
grounding (role preserved / real region mapped / sibling evidence) or the specific violation
(a/b/c/d) and what makes it illegitimate.
Output rules: no preamble before the verdict line — examine the file first, then write.
Your verdict is final and applied automatically, so do not end with a question or an
offer.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision2.v

response

I'll examine the actual file before judging.

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness → file). All seven modules my_fir_f1/f2/f3/h1/h2/h3/h4 remain 7-tap FIR filters: the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, the new_data_rdy gate, the output_data_ready flag, and the enabled 28-bit dout register are all untouched (stereovision2.v:1516-1534 and the parallel blocks). The adapted region is still the tap-sum datapath the witness recorded, not a control path repurposed as a datapath — (c) does not hold.

Real reduction mapped, no fabricated terms. The witness reduction is dout <= (din*COEF0) + (n_delay_reg1*COEF1) + ... + (n_delay_reg6*COEF6), one 28-bit accumulator per module. Every comb_sop_2_18 instance fuses exactly two taps of the same module's own accumulator, with each tap paired to its own coefficient — e.g. stereovision2.v:1493-1509 (f1: taps 0/1, 2/3, 4/5, 6), 1653-1664 (f3: taps 0/1, 2/4, 5/6, skipping the tap whose COEF3_d is 0 at :1620, which the original expression already omits). No operand is imported from another module, another sum, or invented to fill arity — (a) does not hold.

Result drives the original consumers. dout <= sop_sum where sop_sum is the balanced reduction of the block results (:1512-1514, :1667-1668, etc.). dout (28 bits) feeds vidin_out_temp_f1..h4 in h_fltr (:479-499, :514-520), which register into steer_fltr and on to the real_*/imag_* outputs. Nothing is tied to a sink — (b) does not hold.

Bit-exactness checks I ran. WIDTH_5B = 16 and din/n_delay_reg* are unsigned (:1465, :1474, :1481-1486); all live coefficients are positive and ≤ 255 (:1454-1463, :1536-1542, :1617-1623, :1691-1700, :1769-1775, :1848-1854, :1925-1931). So zero-extension to the 18-bit ax/bx and 19-bit ay/by ports preserves value under either a signed or unsigned block interpretation (top bits are 0 → non-negative). Max product 65535×255 ≈ 2^24, seven of them < 2^27, so the 28-bit sop_sum/result[27:0] slice loses nothing relative to the original 28-bit-context sum. The 18×19 asymmetry matches a real sum-of-two-multipliers DSP mode, so the port extension is the natural mapping, not a contrivance.

T2: no precision/algorithm variant introduced, so (d) is not in scope.

Flagged, not disqualifying. Two things a reviewer should see: (1) my_fir_f1 spends a fourth block on the odd seventh tap with bx/by tied to 0 (:1506-1509), while my_fir_f2/my_fir_h2 instead leave the seventh tap as a soft multiply (:1585) — inconsistent packing style, but both are choices a real designer makes for an odd tap count, and the zero-padded half is input padding, not a dead result. (2) The diff also rewrites fltr_compute_f1..h4 (:869, :901, etc.) into a balanced add tree with no mode introduced — gratuitous relative to the witness, but I verified it is bit-exact (all summands are 20-bit sign-extensions into a 20-bit LHS; Verilog context width propagates through parentheses, so reassociation mod 2^20 changes no bit of d_out_tmp).

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 under this run's VPR settings (regime 'koios_small': channel width 300, device koios_small, seed 1) has a critical path of 11.6069 ns and uses 224 DSP tiles of the device's 306, one block per tile. Its used logic-block area is 9.73358e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 1.12977e+09. Its slowest stage is in my_fir_f1: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then six adder levels with their carry chains, then one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop. The next slowest stage, in my_fir_f2 (endpoint group $dffe~642, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 11.293 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. These rules hold for any synchronous design on any FPGA. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone. A term of the original sum that goes on no block port (an addend beside the products) stays outside the block, added to its result. That holds when the module's instantiation ties the term to a constant: the module must compute what it computed for every value of its ports, and the flow flattens the design and removes an add of a constant, so the term puts nothing on the stage and rule 2 does not count it. Fold a term only when it is constant inside the module itself, and say so in the plan.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A block occupies one DSP tile in one mode. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Every DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; a block in either invented mode takes its tile alone.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

    Each module then sums its 3–5 surviving values (block results truncated to [27:0], the plain product, and any shift term) in an explicitly balanced 28-bit tree into the existing enabled dout register.

PLAN:

What changes versus the kept revision 1. Revision 1 put all 41 my_fir products per instance on 132 blocks; in the three modules with an odd product count (f1: 7, f2: 5, h2: 5) that forced one block per module-instance to run with a single product and its second slot tied to 0 — half a DSP tile spent on nothing, 18 half-tiles across the design. This revision drops those 18 half-empty blocks and leaves that one product (f1 reg6*29, f2 reg5*42, h2 reg5*42) as a plain *, which the packer can place two-to-a-tile in two_mult_18x19 mode. Everything else is identical to revision 1. The number of values entering each module's adder tree is unchanged (f1: 4, f2: 5, f3/h1/h3/h4: 3, h2: 5), so every stage keeps exactly the shape and depth that measured 8.70692 ns; only the tile count moves.

Numbers I commit to. 114 blocks, i.e. 114 tiles, and 18 leftover plain multiplies. The design's non-my_fir tiles measured 58 (190 − 132), all in fltr_compute. If the packer pairs the leftovers, 58 + 114 + 9 = 181 tiles; if it pairs none, 58 + 114 + 18 = 190, exactly revision 1's count. I state 184 as the expected landing point (18 leftovers, 3 per instance, typically pack into 2 tiles per instance = 12). Against the unmodified design's 224 tiles that is −40; the device has 306. Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. The invented mode has no chain ports, so partial sums leave the block into general routing and no block ever feeds another; a touched stage is clk-to-Q 0.06 + reg→block 2.25 + block 2.14 + block→soft 1.48 + two adder levels 2.16 ≈ 8.1 ns (f2/h2, with their two shift terms, carry a third level), which is what revision 1 already built and measured below the 8.70692 ns critical path.

The slowest stage, and why this is paid in tiles. The baseline's slowest stage (my_fir_f1: one hard multiply, six adder levels, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 11.6069 ns) was already shortened in revision 1 — the six-level linear chain became a two-level balanced tree over block results, and that soft logic is what left the stage. The critical path now reported is a stage I do not touch: lo15048 → $mul~1235 → lo08017, a bare register → mult_9x9 → register in fltr_compute (qN <= din[..] * const), 0.06 clk-to-Q + 3.505 ns of wire + 2.14 ns of block + 2.983 ns of wire. It holds no soft logic, so replacing that multiply with a block would cost the same 2.14 ns and buy nothing (rule 4), and per rule 1 this revision therefore cannot lower the clock period — it is an area-only change, and I expect the measured period to stay at ~8.7 ns ± placement noise. The gain is 6–9 DSP tiles (≈1.5–2.3e6 area units, ≈2–3%), with a worst case equal to revision 1 rather than worse.

fltr_compute: all 42 candidates per instance left, one reason. fltr_compute_f1/f2/f3/h1/h2/h3/h4 products (regions …859$44 through …1071$134) all stay as they are. Their products already sit four-to-a-tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt on ~58 tiles for 252 products; a comb_sop_2_18 block takes its tile alone for two products, so fusing them would need 126 tiles, +68 over today (rule 6) — about 1.7e7 area units, a ~21% area increase. And it would buy no delay: the stage those products sit on is the reg→mult→reg stage quoted above, which contains no soft arithmetic for a block to absorb (rule 4), while the adder tree that consumes q1..q7 sits on a different, non-critical stage behind a register. Fusing pairs would also mean merging two 17-bit q registers into one partial-sum register; that is latency-neutral and legal, but the tile cost rules it out regardless.

my_fir: three candidates left, one reason. …1492$165 (f1 reg6*29), …1547$178 (f2 reg5*42) and …1721$219 (h2 reg5*42) stay as plain * multiplies precisely because their modules have an odd product count: putting them on a block would leave bx/by tied to 0 and consume a whole tile for one product, whereas as plain multiplies two of them share a tile. They feed the same dout reduction at the same tree level as the block results, so no path lengthens. All other 38 my_fir products per instance are converted.

Preservation of function, inputs, outputs and consumers.

  • Observability. Each converted product feeds only its module's dout sum; nothing else reads a product. The tap registers din/n_delay_reg1..6 keep their own delay-line assignments and remain intact — a block takes a copy of an operand, the signal still exists. output_data_ready and the n_delay_regN shift chain are untouched, so vidin_out_temp_*/my_fir_rdy_* at lines 514–520 and their consumers at 523–551 see identical behaviour.
  • Register control. The blocks are combinational and sit entirely inside the existing if (new_data_rdy) dout <= … stage. No register is moved, added or deleted, so the new_data_rdy enable and the module's one-cycle latency are unchanged (rule 5).
  • Arithmetic exactness. Taps are 16-bit unsigned (WIDTH_5B = 16) and go on 18-bit signed ports, zero-extended — narrower than the port, so exact. Coefficients are positive constants ≤255 on 19-bit signed ports, zero-extended — exact. result[27:0] is taken because the design's dout is a 28-bit register: two's-complement addition is a ring homomorphism mod 2^28, so truncating each partial to 28 bits and summing in 28 bits reproduces the original's low 28 bits bit for bit (and in fact no wrap occurs: the largest tap-weight sum is f2/h2's 673, and 65535×673 ≈ 4.4e7 < 2^28). No product is truncated before the sum in either version. The *4 terms in f2/h2 are terms of the original sum that go on no block port, so they stay outside and are added into the same tree; no term is folded as a constant anywhere.
  • Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. Same multiplies, same sum, same widths, same signedness; only the association of the adder tree and the choice of hard block change, which is what makes this T2 rather than T1.

Risks. (1) Packing: if VPR's packer does not pair the 18 leftover plain multiplies, the design lands at 190 tiles and this revision ties revision 1 instead of beating it — it cannot come out worse on tiles, since 18 half-empty blocks and 18 unpaired multiplies both cost 18 tiles. (2) Seed noise: placement moves the period by ~5.9% on this design, and since the critical path lives in untouched fltr_compute logic, the measured period may drift either way with no causal link to this change; the defensible claim here is the tile count, not the period. (3) The 28-bit truncation of result must be written explicitly in the tree (result[27:0]) so the adders stay 28 bits wide — building the tree in 64-bit context would grow both the carry chains and the LUT count even though the high bits are dead.

Per module (tap t0=din, t1..t6=n_delay_reg1..6), each block is ax={2'b00,tk} (16-bit unsigned tap zero-extended to the signed 18-bit port), ay=COEFk (positive constant ≤255, zero-extended to the signed 19-bit port), bx={2'b00,tm}, by=COEFm; the consumer is result[27:0]:

  • my_fir_f1: B1(ax=din,ay=29; bx=reg1,by=101), B2(ax=reg2,ay=15; bx=reg3,by=235), B3(ax=reg4,ay=15; bx=reg5,by=101); tap reg6*29 (region …1492$165) stays a plain *.

PLAN:

What changes versus the kept revision 1. Revision 1 put all 41 my_fir products per instance on 132 blocks; in the three modules with an odd product count (f1: 7, f2: 5, h2: 5) that forced one block per module-instance to run with a single product and its second slot tied to 0 — half a DSP tile spent on nothing, 18 half-tiles across the design. This revision drops those 18 half-empty blocks and leaves that one product (f1 reg6*29, f2 reg5*42, h2 reg5*42) as a plain *, which the packer can place two-to-a-tile in two_mult_18x19 mode. Everything else is identical to revision 1. The number of values entering each module's adder tree is unchanged (f1: 4, f2: 5, f3/h1/h3/h4: 3, h2: 5), so every stage keeps exactly the shape and depth that measured 8.70692 ns; only the tile count moves.

Numbers I commit to. 114 blocks, i.e. 114 tiles, and 18 leftover plain multiplies. The design's non-my_fir tiles measured 58 (190 − 132), all in fltr_compute. If the packer pairs the leftovers, 58 + 114 + 9 = 181 tiles; if it pairs none, 58 + 114 + 18 = 190, exactly revision 1's count. I state 184 as the expected landing point (18 leftovers, 3 per instance, typically pack into 2 tiles per instance = 12). Against the unmodified design's 224 tiles that is −40; the device has 306. Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. The invented mode has no chain ports, so partial sums leave the block into general routing and no block ever feeds another; a touched stage is clk-to-Q 0.06 + reg→block 2.25 + block 2.14 + block→soft 1.48 + two adder levels 2.16 ≈ 8.1 ns (f2/h2, with their two shift terms, carry a third level), which is what revision 1 already built and measured below the 8.70692 ns critical path.

The slowest stage, and why this is paid in tiles. The baseline's slowest stage (my_fir_f1: one hard multiply, six adder levels, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 11.6069 ns) was already shortened in revision 1 — the six-level linear chain became a two-level balanced tree over block results, and that soft logic is what left the stage. The critical path now reported is a stage I do not touch: lo15048 → $mul~1235 → lo08017, a bare register → mult_9x9 → register in fltr_compute (qN <= din[..] * const), 0.06 clk-to-Q + 3.505 ns of wire + 2.14 ns of block + 2.983 ns of wire. It holds no soft logic, so replacing that multiply with a block would cost the same 2.14 ns and buy nothing (rule 4), and per rule 1 this revision therefore cannot lower the clock period — it is an area-only change, and I expect the measured period to stay at ~8.7 ns ± placement noise. The gain is 6–9 DSP tiles (≈1.5–2.3e6 area units, ≈2–3%), with a worst case equal to revision 1 rather than worse.

fltr_compute: all 42 candidates per instance left, one reason. fltr_compute_f1/f2/f3/h1/h2/h3/h4 products (regions …859$44 through …1071$134) all stay as they are. Their products already sit four-to-a-tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt on ~58 tiles for 252 products; a comb_sop_2_18 block takes its tile alone for two products, so fusing them would need 126 tiles, +68 over today (rule 6) — about 1.7e7 area units, a ~21% area increase. And it would buy no delay: the stage those products sit on is the reg→mult→reg stage quoted above, which contains no soft arithmetic for a block to absorb (rule 4), while the adder tree that consumes q1..q7 sits on a different, non-critical stage behind a register. Fusing pairs would also mean merging two 17-bit q registers into one partial-sum register; that is latency-neutral and legal, but the tile cost rules it out regardless.

my_fir: three candidates left, one reason. …1492$165 (f1 reg6*29), …1547$178 (f2 reg5*42) and …1721$219 (h2 reg5*42) stay as plain * multiplies precisely because their modules have an odd product count: putting them on a block would leave bx/by tied to 0 and consume a whole tile for one product, whereas as plain multiplies two of them share a tile. They feed the same dout reduction at the same tree level as the block results, so no path lengthens. All other 38 my_fir products per instance are converted.

Preservation of function, inputs, outputs and consumers.

  • Observability. Each converted product feeds only its module's dout sum; nothing else reads a product. The tap registers din/n_delay_reg1..6 keep their own delay-line assignments and remain intact — a block takes a copy of an operand, the signal still exists. output_data_ready and the n_delay_regN shift chain are untouched, so vidin_out_temp_*/my_fir_rdy_* at lines 514–520 and their consumers at 523–551 see identical behaviour.
  • Register control. The blocks are combinational and sit entirely inside the existing if (new_data_rdy) dout <= … stage. No register is moved, added or deleted, so the new_data_rdy enable and the module's one-cycle latency are unchanged (rule 5).
  • Arithmetic exactness. Taps are 16-bit unsigned (WIDTH_5B = 16) and go on 18-bit signed ports, zero-extended — narrower than the port, so exact. Coefficients are positive constants ≤255 on 19-bit signed ports, zero-extended — exact. result[27:0] is taken because the design's dout is a 28-bit register: two's-complement addition is a ring homomorphism mod 2^28, so truncating each partial to 28 bits and summing in 28 bits reproduces the original's low 28 bits bit for bit (and in fact no wrap occurs: the largest tap-weight sum is f2/h2's 673, and 65535×673 ≈ 4.4e7 < 2^28). No product is truncated before the sum in either version. The *4 terms in f2/h2 are terms of the original sum that go on no block port, so they stay outside and are added into the same tree; no term is folded as a constant anywhere.
  • Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. Same multiplies, same sum, same widths, same signedness; only the association of the adder tree and the choice of hard block change, which is what makes this T2 rather than T1.

Risks. (1) Packing: if VPR's packer does not pair the 18 leftover plain multiplies, the design lands at 190 tiles and this revision ties revision 1 instead of beating it — it cannot come out worse on tiles, since 18 half-empty blocks and 18 unpaired multiplies both cost 18 tiles. (2) Seed noise: placement moves the period by ~5.9% on this design, and since the critical path lives in untouched fltr_compute logic, the measured period may drift either way with no causal link to this change; the defensible claim here is the tile count, not the period. (3) The 28-bit truncation of result must be written explicitly in the tree (result[27:0]) so the adders stay 28 bits wide — building the tree in 64-bit context would grow both the carry chains and the LUT count even though the high bits are dead.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision2.v

response

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verified against /work/stereovision2.v:

  • Regions are real and correctly mapped. All 38 selected regions are actual products in the seven my_fir_* dout reductions. Coefficients match the source exactly: f1 29/101/15/235/15/101/29 (stereovision2.v:1440-1449), f2 & h2 4/42/163/255/163/42/4, f3 12/77/148/0/148/77/12, h1 15/25/193/0/193/25/15, h3 & h4 9/56/109/0/109/56/9. The modules where COEF3 == 0 (f3, h1, h3, h4) genuinely omit the n_delay_reg3 term from the sum (stereovision2.v:1602-1607, 1661-1666, 1775-1780, 1833-1838), and the plan's pairings correctly skip it.
  • (c) no fabricated terms. Every one of the 19 blocks per instance carries two distinct products drawn from the same module's single dout reduction; no slot is padded. The three leftovers (f1 …1492$165, f2 …1547$178, h2 …1721$219) are explicitly left as plain * rather than half-filling a block — the odd-count arithmetic (7, 7, 7 products with two *4 shifts in f2/h2) checks out.
  • (d) no mode island. Operands are the live din/n_delay_reg* taps; results feed the existing enabled 28-bit dout, which drives vidin_out_temp_* → vidin_out_reg_* → steer_fltr (stereovision2.v:514-521, 523-553). Constants appear only on the coefficient operand, which is the normal SOP form, not a dead/constant sink.
  • (a) function preserved. WIDTH_5B = 16, taps unsigned, coefficients positive ≤ 255, so zero-extension onto the 18/19-bit signed ports is exact. The reassociation into a balanced 28-bit tree is sound for integer addition, and the mod-2^28 truncation argument holds — the largest weighted sum is f2/h2's 673 × 65535 ≈ 4.4e7 < 2^28, so no wrap even occurs. No register added, moved, or removed; the new_data_rdy enable and one-cycle latency are intact, as is the n_delay_reg shift chain.
  • (b) no new op/datatype family. Same multiplies, same adds, same widths and signedness; only association and block choice change — within T2.

Instance count confirms the committed numbers: h_fltr is instantiated 6 times, so 19 blocks × 6 = 114 blocks and 3 × 6 = 18 leftover plain multiplies, consistent with the 184-tile estimate.

The delay claims (no period improvement, value delivered as tile/area reduction) are stated honestly with their packing and seed-noise risks, and none of them constitute a violation under (a)–(d).

PLAN:

What changes versus the kept revision 1. Revision 1 put all 41 my_fir products per instance on 132 blocks; in the three modules with an odd product count (f1: 7, f2: 5, h2: 5) that forced one block per module-instance to run with a single product and its second slot tied to 0 — half a DSP tile spent on nothing, 18 half-tiles across the design. This revision drops those 18 half-empty blocks and leaves that one product (f1 reg6*29, f2 reg5*42, h2 reg5*42) as a plain *, which the packer can place two-to-a-tile in two_mult_18x19 mode. Everything else is identical to revision 1. The number of values entering each module's adder tree is unchanged (f1: 4, f2: 5, f3/h1/h3/h4: 3, h2: 5), so every stage keeps exactly the shape and depth that measured 8.70692 ns; only the tile count moves.

Numbers I commit to. 114 blocks, i.e. 114 tiles, and 18 leftover plain multiplies. The design's non-my_fir tiles measured 58 (190 − 132), all in fltr_compute. If the packer pairs the leftovers, 58 + 114 + 9 = 181 tiles; if it pairs none, 58 + 114 + 18 = 190, exactly revision 1's count. I state 184 as the expected landing point (18 leftovers, 3 per instance, typically pack into 2 tiles per instance = 12). Against the unmodified design's 224 tiles that is −40; the device has 306. Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. The invented mode has no chain ports, so partial sums leave the block into general routing and no block ever feeds another; a touched stage is clk-to-Q 0.06 + reg→block 2.25 + block 2.14 + block→soft 1.48 + two adder levels 2.16 ≈ 8.1 ns (f2/h2, with their two shift terms, carry a third level), which is what revision 1 already built and measured below the 8.70692 ns critical path.

The slowest stage, and why this is paid in tiles. The baseline's slowest stage (my_fir_f1: one hard multiply, six adder levels, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 11.6069 ns) was already shortened in revision 1 — the six-level linear chain became a two-level balanced tree over block results, and that soft logic is what left the stage. The critical path now reported is a stage I do not touch: lo15048 → $mul~1235 → lo08017, a bare register → mult_9x9 → register in fltr_compute (qN <= din[..] * const), 0.06 clk-to-Q + 3.505 ns of wire + 2.14 ns of block + 2.983 ns of wire. It holds no soft logic, so replacing that multiply with a block would cost the same 2.14 ns and buy nothing (rule 4), and per rule 1 this revision therefore cannot lower the clock period — it is an area-only change, and I expect the measured period to stay at ~8.7 ns ± placement noise. The gain is 6–9 DSP tiles (≈1.5–2.3e6 area units, ≈2–3%), with a worst case equal to revision 1 rather than worse.

fltr_compute: all 42 candidates per instance left, one reason. fltr_compute_f1/f2/f3/h1/h2/h3/h4 products (regions …859$44 through …1071$134) all stay as they are. Their products already sit four-to-a-tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt on ~58 tiles for 252 products; a comb_sop_2_18 block takes its tile alone for two products, so fusing them would need 126 tiles, +68 over today (rule 6) — about 1.7e7 area units, a ~21% area increase. And it would buy no delay: the stage those products sit on is the reg→mult→reg stage quoted above, which contains no soft arithmetic for a block to absorb (rule 4), while the adder tree that consumes q1..q7 sits on a different, non-critical stage behind a register. Fusing pairs would also mean merging two 17-bit q registers into one partial-sum register; that is latency-neutral and legal, but the tile cost rules it out regardless.

my_fir: three candidates left, one reason. …1492$165 (f1 reg6*29), …1547$178 (f2 reg5*42) and …1721$219 (h2 reg5*42) stay as plain * multiplies precisely because their modules have an odd product count: putting them on a block would leave bx/by tied to 0 and consume a whole tile for one product, whereas as plain multiplies two of them share a tile. They feed the same dout reduction at the same tree level as the block results, so no path lengthens. All other 38 my_fir products per instance are converted.

Preservation of function, inputs, outputs and consumers.

  • Observability. Each converted product feeds only its module's dout sum; nothing else reads a product. The tap registers din/n_delay_reg1..6 keep their own delay-line assignments and remain intact — a block takes a copy of an operand, the signal still exists. output_data_ready and the n_delay_regN shift chain are untouched, so vidin_out_temp_*/my_fir_rdy_* at lines 514–520 and their consumers at 523–551 see identical behaviour.
  • Register control. The blocks are combinational and sit entirely inside the existing if (new_data_rdy) dout <= … stage. No register is moved, added or deleted, so the new_data_rdy enable and the module's one-cycle latency are unchanged (rule 5).
  • Arithmetic exactness. Taps are 16-bit unsigned (WIDTH_5B = 16) and go on 18-bit signed ports, zero-extended — narrower than the port, so exact. Coefficients are positive constants ≤255 on 19-bit signed ports, zero-extended — exact. result[27:0] is taken because the design's dout is a 28-bit register: two's-complement addition is a ring homomorphism mod 2^28, so truncating each partial to 28 bits and summing in 28 bits reproduces the original's low 28 bits bit for bit (and in fact no wrap occurs: the largest tap-weight sum is f2/h2's 673, and 65535×673 ≈ 4.4e7 < 2^28). No product is truncated before the sum in either version. The *4 terms in f2/h2 are terms of the original sum that go on no block port, so they stay outside and are added into the same tree; no term is folded as a constant anywhere.
  • Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. Same multiplies, same sum, same widths, same signedness; only the association of the adder tree and the choice of hard block change, which is what makes this T2 rather than T1.

Risks. (1) Packing: if VPR's packer does not pair the 18 leftover plain multiplies, the design lands at 190 tiles and this revision ties revision 1 instead of beating it — it cannot come out worse on tiles, since 18 half-empty blocks and 18 unpaired multiplies both cost 18 tiles. (2) Seed noise: placement moves the period by ~5.9% on this design, and since the critical path lives in untouched fltr_compute logic, the measured period may drift either way with no causal link to this change; the defensible claim here is the tile count, not the period. (3) The 28-bit truncation of result must be written explicitly in the tree (result[27:0]) so the adders stay 28 bits wide — building the tree in 64-bit context would grow both the carry chains and the LUT count even though the high bits are dead.

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
3	# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
4	# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
5	# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
6	# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
7	# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
8	# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
9	# with its instance count.
10	set -u
11	f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
12	out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
13	rc=$?
14	if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
15	  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
16	  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
17	  echo "$out" | tail -10
18	  exit 1
19	fi
20	echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
21	echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
22	echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
23	                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
24	                   on {on=0}
25	                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
26	  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

prompt

You are an INDEPENDENT ACCEPTANCE REVIEWER — the gate of record for whether an adapted design is
a LEGITIMATE member of its benchmark suite. You did NOT plan or write this edit. Judge only what
is in front of you. Your verdict is final and fully automated; there is no human backstop, so err
toward rejecting anything you cannot positively ground.

FRAMING — refute by default. Your job is to find why this adaptation is NOT a legitimate suite
member. Assume it is contrived until the evidence shows otherwise. A rewrite that merely "makes
the mode appear in the netlist" is NOT sufficient — the mode must do real work the original
workload actually implied.

This case has already passed the deterministic structural, provenance, and (for T3) evidence
gates — do not re-litigate those mechanically. Your judgement is the legitimacy call they
cannot make: would a real designer of THIS workload ship this, or is it a mode-island / fabricated
dot-product / gratuitous variant dressed up to pass?

REJECT if any of these hold:

  • (a) FABRICATED TERMS — a fused N-term product/sum aggregates operands that did not genuinely
    share a reduction in the original; the "dot product" is manufactured to fill the mode's arity.
  • (b) MODE ISLAND / DEAD WORK — the mode's result does not flow to the design's real outputs, or
    is wired to constants / a sink that the rest of the design ignores.
  • (c) ROLE BREAK — the adapted region no longer plays the workload role the witness recorded
    (e.g. a control-path multiply repurposed as a fake datapath MAC).
  • (d) UNGROUNDED VARIANT (T3) — the precision/algorithm variant is not the one the cited sibling
    design evidences; the adaptation drifts to a workload character the suite does not contain.

ACCEPT if the adaptation is sound: the mapped arithmetic genuinely existed, the fused terms really
shared a reduction, the mode's output drives the original consumers, and the workload role is
preserved. Legitimate adaptation need not be optimal — only real.

You MUST ground your verdict: cite the specific preserved role (from the witness), the real
region(s)/reduction the mode maps onto, and — for T3 — the sibling evidence axis. A verdict you
cannot ground is a REJECT.

  • // Four taps of the dout reduction are fused onto two comb_sop_2_18 blocks
  • // (result = ax*ay + bx*by); taps zero-extended to the signed 18-bit ax/bx ports,

grounding (role preserved / real region mapped / sibling evidence) or the specific violation
(a/b/c/d) and what makes it illegitimate.

response

I'll examine the file before rendering a verdict.

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness → file). The witness records role my_fir_f1…h4, operation sop, with the shared reduction being "the dout <= (din*COEF0) + … + (n_delay_reg6*COEF6) sum in each my_fir_* module — seven distinct reductions, one per module." That is exactly what survives: every edited module is still a 7-tap delay-line FIR, the tap chain (n_delay_reg1..6) is untouched, the reduction is still evaluated in the same always @(posedge clk) / new_data_rdy guarded stage, still assigned to the same 28-bit dout, with output_data_ready unchanged. No control-path logic was recruited into a datapath MAC — the multiplies fused are the FIR tap multiplies themselves. (c) does not hold.

Real reduction mapped, no fabricated terms. Each comb_sop_2_18 fuses exactly two operand pairs that were already addends of the same module's dout sum — e.g. stereovision2.v:1487-1495 pairs (din·COEF0_b, reg1·COEF1_b), (reg2·COEF2_b, reg3·COEF3_b), (reg4·COEF4_b, reg5·COEF5_b), all six of which are terms of the single f1 reduction. There is no cross-module or cross-reduction pairing anywhere (f1/f2/f3/h1/h2/h3/h4 each keep their own coefficient set and their own taps). The arity also lines up with the witness rather than being padded to it: 3+2+3+3+2+3+3 = 19 blocks × 2 = 38 multiplies, matching the 38 $mul regions the witness lists, module-by-module (f1 6→3, f2 4→2, f3 6→3, h1 6→3, h2 4→2, h3 6→3, h4 6→3). The taps left in soft logic are the ones a designer would leave: f2/h2 COEF0/COEF6 = 4 (a shift) at stereovision2.v:1578-1580 and 1800-1802, and f3/h1/h3/h4 omit COEF3 = 0, which the original source already omitted. (a) does not hold.

Not a mode island. The sop outputs are the sole source of dout's value — dout <= (sop0[27:0] + sop1[27:0]) + sop2[27:0] (e.g. stereovision2.v:1952), no constants, no parallel shadow copy of the old expression, no unused sink. dout flows out unchanged through vidin_out_temp_f1 → vidin_out_reg_f1 (stereovision2.v:514, 527) into steer_fltr (stereovision2.v:521) and on to the design's real outputs. The intended consumers in the manifest are the same seven modules the witness recorded. (b) does not hold.

T2 / no variant. Manifest introduces no operations or datatypes, and the file bears that out: taps stay 16-bit (WIDTH_5B = 5'b10000), zero-extended by two bits onto the signed 18-bit ax/bx ports, and coefficients are positive constants ≤ 255 zero-extended onto the 19-bit ay/by ports, so the signed ports carry non-negative values and the arithmetic is value-identical. The [27:0] slice of the 64-bit result is lossless (16b × 8b summed in pairs ≤ 25 bits), and the final tree is the same 28-bit truncating accumulation the original dout performed. (d) is not in scope for T2 and no precision/algorithm drift is present.

One imprecision worth noting, non-blocking: the f2/h2 comments (stereovision2.v:1551-1552, 1773-1774) describe the leftover COEF5 tap as "the odd COEF5_c tap" while COEF5_c is 42 — the same value fused as COEF1_c. The comment reads as "left over," not a claim about parity; the netlist semantics are unaffected.

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 through the same flow under the koios_small settings regime (channel width 300, device koios_small, seed 1, grid 140 x 140 (koios_small), routed at width 300); measured, 2026-09-15 00:15:24 -0700, from configs/baselines/complexDSP.yaml. A lower delta is better on every row.

Used logic block area: VPR's "Total used logic block area" (minimum-width transistor areas, I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded). Area x critical path: 7.05793e+08 against the baseline's 1.12977e+09 (-37.53%).

Worst reg-to-reg: the slowest path of the flow's report_timing.setup.rpt that starts and ends on a register (neither end an I/O pad), as the period that path alone needs (capture edge minus launch edge minus slack; for path 1 this is the critical path to three decimals). This run: 8.707 ns, path 1: lo15048 to lo08017, clock tm3_clk_v0, module not identified (plain multiply $mul~1235[0]: a 8-bit signal, b 91, out 54-bit signal). Path 1 is itself register to register, so this is the critical path. Offered and packed are not the same unit: a site is a source region, a packed block is an elaborated instance, and one block can fuse several sites (or the editor can chain several blocks for one site), so they are recorded raw rather than as a ratio.

revision 1
baseline
regime koios_small · used area 9.73358e+07 · critical path 11.6069 ns · area x delay 1.12977e+09 (read from entry)

The objective is the area-delay product: VPR's "Total used logic block area" (minimum-width transistor areas, I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path, each the median over the seed list taken independently. In search mode the step fires whenever there is a product to improve on, whether or not it beat the baseline.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period and area over the seeds. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
